# Follow-up review register for `6586b7a`

## Purpose

This register closes the independent review of `rskrb5-ng` at `6586b7a`. Every
finding, nit included, ends with a final status backed by a proof.

## Method

Review sources: the Oracle cross-cutting review, six unit critics, the revmux
runs on the strongest units (u2-u6) and unit u1 run 02, the worker's own
leftover notes, and the mutation-testing artifacts. A planner consolidated them
into a ledger of canonical rows (`FU-<AREA>-<NN>`). The ledger is not committed;
this register is self-contained. Revmux finder availability and degraded-round
limits are recorded under FU-OPS-03.

## Status vocabulary

- `open`: not yet acted on (initial state of every row).
- `fixed`: fixed with a named proof (a failing-first test or a check) and commit.
- `dismissed`: shown not to be a defect; evidence recorded.
- `transferred`: a parity feature moved to a named plan-2 todo.

Todo 53 dependency admission: the owner approved `syn` on 2026-11-11 and
the existing `proc-macro2` 1.0.107 dev edge solely for `span-locations` on
the same date (option 1). `Cargo.toml` records both approvals. No new locked
package or runtime dependency was added; before/after
`cargo tree -e normal --all-features` outputs are byte-identical. Deny
(including licenses), vet, audit, machete and Taplo remain required;
`docs/lint-policy.md` records the runtime-table dependency-diff rule and
the absence of a separate committed SBOM.

Executor decisions of 2026-11-11 (not owner dependency approvals): literal
`include!` targets are parsed relative to their including file with bounded
cycle/depth checks. Only locally defined macro transcribers proven to emit
impls or declaration-free expressions are accepted; opaque/external item
invocations fail closed. The inventory scope changes from a directory walk
to the library module graph rooted at `src/lib.rs`. Every library feature
branch remains present; only structurally proven test-only items, fields
and module files are excluded, and ambiguous cfg syntax rejects. This
keeps test-support `thread_local!`/`proptest!` outside the library owner
inventory without a filename or macro-name allowlist. Production sources
are unchanged. Earlier FU-TST-R21..R25 fixtures remain, with the four
resolvable sibling/parent-child alias cases upgraded from ambiguity errors
to exact secret-owner assertions on the same inputs and CRLF variants.

an explicit address TCP, then UDP), UDP only after a definite TCP failure, one datagram per endpoint without retransmit, `udp_preference_limit` ignored; a UDP refusal (`ConnectionRefused`, or `ConnectionReset` on Windows) is a plain endpoint failure (`KdcEndpointFailures`, or `Client::Io` for an explicit address) and no longer `KpasswdOutcomeUnknown`; an uncertain TCP outcome never leads to UDP. v0.2.2 went UDP first and fell back to TCP after any UDP error. Kept divergences from MIT: no UDP after an uncertain TCP outcome; no UDP retransmit; every failure after the datagram except a refusal stays unknown (MIT-admin-1). | FU-SPN-R20 | minor | `NegTokenInit::decode` rejected the whole token when the optional `reqFlags` BIT STRING was not canonical named-bit DER or exceeded 32 bits (`00 80`, `01 80`, `01 04`, `01 00`, `00 00`, `07 02`, more than 32 bits), where RFC 4178 section 4.2.1 and MS-SPNG 3.1.5.5 say the acceptor MUST ignore `reqFlags` and v0.2.2 accepted any bytes (PD-05, closed by owner decision 2026-11-13 "strict encode, lenient decode") | fixed | `src/spnego/field_tests.rs`: `req_flags_decoding_keeps_noncanonical_named_bits` (decode OK for 8 non-canonical contents with the bytes kept, `mech_types`, `mech_token` and `mech_list_mic` intact, and re-encoding returning `Spnego::InvalidReqFlags`) and the decode half of `req_flags_reject_malformed_bit_strings` red first on the unfixed tree (target t110-red; both fail with `Error: Spnego(Discriminant(6), ..)`, the `InvalidReqFlags` returned by decode), then green; `req_flags_decoding_requires_bit_string_framing` (truncated length, trailing byte inside `[1]`, OCTET STRING tag, constructed tag, indefinite length, empty `[1]` are still errors), `req_flags_encoding_rejects_noncanonical_named_bits` and `req_flags_canonical_named_bits_roundtrip` pass on both trees and pin the unchanged encode rule and round trip; RFC 4178 4.2.1; MIT 1.22.2 `spnego_mech.c:3394-3403` `get_req_flags` is stricter (only `03 02 01 xx`, else `GSS_S_DEFECTIVE_TOKEN`) and the value is unused afterwards: the lenient decode is a deliberate divergence from MIT, per RFC 4178 4.2.1 and MS-SPNG 3.1.5.5 (FU-DOC-R110) | CHANGELOG [0.3.0] bullet FU-SPN-R20: `NegTokenInit::decode` keeps the `reqFlags` contents as received and no longer fails the token for them; encoding still returns `Spnego::InvalidReqFlags` for a value that is not canonical named-bit DER of at most 32 bits and never canonicalises, so a lenient-decoded non-canonical value fails to re-encode with the same error. Decode still requires the `[1]` field to wrap one well-formed BIT STRING TLV (tag 0x03, definite DER length that fits, nothing after it); the contents, including an empty one, are not checked. v0.2.2 accepted any bytes on decode and encode.
